# vanilla-plus — HISTORY.md

*Companion archive to `handoff.md`, split out in **rev 7 (2026-10-27)**. Everything here is closed, superseded, or record-keeping: finished fix series, retired infrastructure, deep diagnostic specs and closed investigations. Nothing in this file is needed to execute current or upcoming tasks — but nothing in it was deleted, because it is the only durable record of how the current state was reached. Read it when a "why is it like this" question has no answer in handoff.md. Companion journal: `/home/z/my-project/worklog.md` (worklog wins on disagreement).*

*Split point: vanilla-plus tip `4857c30` ("cleanup diag") + this commit. At split time the sound-defect series was closed, upstreamed as jaenster/redivivus PR #10, and publicly released as `D2LoD-Vanilla-Plus-0.0.1`.*
*Second offload: rev 16 (2026-10-28, repo restructure executed — fork `main` is now the former `vanilla-plus` line, `libslang-vk` branch renamed `Glide3xVK`). handoff revs 8–13 → H20; fix-chain ledger rows 1–27 → H21; the ad964f3 supersession map → H22; the naming-collision table + pr2-drift note → H23. Split point: `main` tip `81a084e` + this commit. At second-split time `D2LoD-Vanilla-Plus-0.0.3` was out (`569fc5c`) and the d2boot.log growth fix had retired the last unbounded probes.*
*Third restructure: 2026-11-03, the repository is renamed **`D2LoD-libslang-vk` → `D2VP-QoL`** and the wrapper line leaves as its own repository, **[`VTSTech/Glide3xVK`](https://github.com/VTSTech/Glide3xVK)** (formerly the `Glide3xVK` branch, itself the former `libslang-vk`; 0.4.6 latest). The H-records below are verbatim archives and keep the names and URLs they carried at the time — old GitHub URLs redirect to the renamed repo; current-state docs (README, handoff, PLAN, VANILLA-PLUS, CHANGELOG) are updated.*
*Docs restructure: **2026-11-06 (rev 135)** — the owner's human/agent doc split: README.md + CHANGELOG.md are the human lane (concise by policy), plans/ is the agent lane (all plans), handoff.md carries a hard **16 KB budget** (gate-enforced, `tools/check-vanilla-plus.sh`), HISTORY.md stays the unbounded verbatim archive. The pre-restructure CHANGELOG / handoff / SMOKE texts live in **H41 / H42 / H43**.

## H3. The sound-defect series — full record (2026-10-26/27, CLOSED + upstreamed)

Two defects after the eleven; retail 1.14d Game.exe's sound code (the user's Ghidra decompiles) is the reference throughout. Status at archive time: both fixed on vanilla-plus, both shipped upstream via **jaenster/redivivus PR #10** (branch `pr4/sound-except-intro`, 3 commits, merge-clean at filing).

**Defect 12 — title/in-game music silent (CLOSED, field-verified).** Intro-video (Bink) audio was always audible — separate path. Every DDA/SFile music buffer sat at DSBVOLUME_MIN (-10000) from its first feed. Root cause: **Ghidra prints double constants as IEEE-754 bit patterns, and the port transcribed them as hex *integer* literals.** `0x3f1a36e2e0000000` is the double `0.0001` (the clamp epsilon — as an integer ≈7.3e19), `0x409f400000000000` is `2000.0` (the centibel scale); pasted as integers, `SOUNDSMP_ClampVolume`'s `value <= min + eps` fired on every input and every conversion returned -10000. The R5 scale fix (-2000.0) sat *behind* that clamp and never executed — which is why the fcd50d8/ce32a18 rounds changed nothing. Fixed five literals in `D2Sound/Src/D2SoundSmp.cpp` (two ClampVolume epsilons; ClampAttenuation epsilon, bottom bound, divisor — the old bottom bound `(double)-0x3f3c780000000000` actually decodes to -0.0004344, a third casualty; `-10000.0` is the retail curve's real bottom). Commit `d86ba98` = user's `4031fa7` "R6 fix". Field: "Music works now! Both at title and in game."

Retail volume-curve reference (user-supplied FUN_005165f0), now reproduced exactly: `value ≤ min+0.0001 → -10000`; `value ≥ max-0.0001 → 0`; else `-2000·log10((max+min)/value)` centibels. Verified: 255→0, 110→-730, 0→-10000; `SOUNDSMP_ClampAttenuation` (10^(att/2000)) is its exact inverse. DirectSound: volume -10000 = absolute mute, 0 = full; hr 0 = SetVolume succeeded.

**Defect 13 — NPC speech + monster/player SFX silent (CLOSED — fix 16 `9c8beb9`, field-verified).** Symptom was: "Still no NPC Speech or Monster/Player sounds." R6 log ground truth: Warriv speech **does** stream+play (`DATA\LOCAL\SFX\common\warriv\war_goodday.wav`, dda PLAY#3) but post-open GetVolume = -10000 → the **client-side** volume chain writes silence. Music is unaffected (own volume source). Client chain: `SOUND_PlaySound` (D2Client/Sound/Sound.cpp:5441, request) → tick → `SOUND_PlaySoundWithPanning` (GAME/Sound.cpp:634) → `SOUND_UpdateChannelParams` (GAME/Sound.cpp:410, volume) → SetVolume/OpenAndStreamFile.

**The SMP layer is EXONERATED (2026-10-27).** User supplied retail decompiles of all four FUN_005165f0 callers — FUN_005157b0 SmpSetVolume, FUN_00515890 SmpSetPan, FUN_00515a90 SmpSetEaxObstruction, FUN_00515b40 SmpSetEaxRoomLevel — plus the complete call-site list (5 sites inside the same four functions). The port matches retail on every axis: halt IDs (0x2cb/0x2ef/0x345/0x359), `D2SoundSmpStrc` offsets (pDSBuffer@0x00, pEaxInterface@0x10, dwFlags byte@0x34 bit 2, nVolume@0x38, fEaxObstruction float@0x3c, size 0x60), float literals (0x437f0000=255.0f, 0x42fe0000=127.0f, 0x3f800000=1.0f), the master multiply `gnSoundGlobalVolume × vol / 255` with store-to-nVolume, the quality-flag==2 attenuation skip, and EAX property IDs 7 / 1 / 5+4 with 4-byte payloads. **Do not re-audit the SMP layer.**

**Resolution (R7 → R8 → fix 16).** R7 log (title + Act 1 town): every non-stream sound's `updchan:` ended `vol=0` while its inputs were non-zero (b1c 125–255, svol 90–255, even an mvol=1/b38=2 channel) → SetVolume att=-10000; music escaped via the dedicated stream+fade path (`fadein:`/`fadevol:` ramps; town1 sid=4673 landed att=-1348 = att of its pre-Calc volume, exactly as the retail curve predicts). R8 log (floats re-logged as IEEE bit patterns after R7's `%f` printed literal `f`): the client's PRE-Calc volume was **healthy** (mv=100 muv=50 lcv=100 rcv=100; pre-Calc vol 125–220), and war_goodday.wav (sid=4325) landed att=-10000 through an open path that writes no volume — pinning the zero inside the single `SOUNDSMP_CalculateVolumeAndPan` call site (the `if (!pSoundChannel[1])` block in `SOUND_UpdateChannelParams`). **Root cause:** the call passed `(uint32_t*)&fFalloffMin / &fFalloffMax` — the *storage of two float locals* — and the block read the results back AS FLOATS: `(int)fFalloffMin` for the volume multiply, `(int32_t)fFalloffMax` for SetPan. Any CalcVol in 0..255 has IEEE bits `0x000000xx`, which reinterprets as a subnormal float (~1e-43) and truncates to 0 → volume 0 for EVERY channel taking the block; music-flagged channels (`pSoundChannel[1] != 0`) skip it — exactly the observed music-vs-SFX split. **Fix 16** (`D2Client/GAME/Sound.cpp`): receive the out params as proper `uint32_t nCalcVolume / nCalcPan` locals and use them for `SOUNDSMP_SetPan` and the `nCalcVolume * preVol / 0xff` multiply — retail integer semantics, no float round-trip. Field: "The sounds works now. NPC's Monsters, Player. All working."

**Known open deviation (carried forward as a handoff watch item): stereo position only, NOT a silence cause.** Our `SOUNDSMP_SetPan` right-branch negates (`SetPan((int)-fVar1)`); retail as-decompiled passes the raw (negative) conversion result to vtable+0x40. Retail's slot may be a wrapper Ghidra flattened — identify the retail owner before touching; do not "fix" blind.

**Upstream record (2026-10-27).** Issue + PR filed on jaenster/redivivus: **PR #10** ("Sound: only the intro video had audio — bind DirectSound to the game window, decode FP bit-pattern constants, read CalculateVolumeAndPan's integer out params as integers"), head `VTSTech:pr4/sound-except-intro` → base `main` @ `eef5f2c`, 3 commits / 3 files, `mergeable_state: clean` at filing. The 3 upstream commits carry self-contained comments (no vanilla-plus/sounddiag jargon): (1) hwnd fix `Application.cpp`; (2) FP constant decode + `-2000.0` scale restore in `D2SoundSmp.cpp` (upstream ALSO lacked the R5 scale fix — its `ClampVolume` still had `-0x3f60c00000000000` = −0.00341796875); (3) integer out params in `GAME/Sound.cpp`. Patches + drafts archived in the sandbox as `download/pr4-sound-except-intro{,.zip}` / `download/redivivus-{issue,pr}-sound-except-intro.md`. GAP at filing: the PR body is missing its `Fixes #N` line (must be re-added or the issue closed manually on merge).

## H4. Diagnostic deep specs (modules still in-tree unless marked retired)

- **ShadowDiag** (`D2Glide/Renderer/ShadowDiag.{h,cpp}` + 3 hooks in Glide.cpp: include, `DrawGroundTile` head `NoteWorldFrame()`, `DrawShadowQuad` submit-tail `OnShadowQuad`): arms at the first ground tile (in-world marker), captures the **first 24 in-world shadow quads**, writes `d2shadow-diag.txt` **once per session**. Record layout: unit/frame identity, DC6 block header, full `GlideTexInfoStrc` (nAspect@+0x34, fScale0/fTexS0/fTexT0/fTexS1@+0x20..+0x2c), mask crc32 + ASCII bitmap, 4 fan vertices (28-byte stride). Quirk: cache-hit records show stale mask crc/bitmap — `UploadTextureToCache`'s cache-hit path early-outs *without* touching `gpGlideSpriteBuffer` (Glide.cpp ~1117–1123), so draw-time reads of the shared decode buffer show the previous sprite's content on cache hits. Not a render bug — only first-decode records are trustworthy.
- **LightDiag v2** (`D2Client/Draw/LightDiag.{h,cpp}` + hooks in `LightMap.cpp` (AllocLightMap inputs), `D2Client.cpp:1802` (OBJECT_SetLightRGB with raw `objects.txt` row bytes 0x160..0x16F), `Draw.cpp`): dumps `d2light-diag.txt` **once at in-world frame 90** (v2 moved it from frame 2 so world-init object lights fit the budget). Decoder: `scripts/parse_light_diag.py` (asserts reader==txt, blue-zero scans, packed-vs-readback round-trip; exit 1 on any anomaly).
- **sounddiag (RETIRED, r10, 2026-10-27)** — was `D2BOOT_MarkFmt("[sounddiag] …")` lines into `d2boot.log`. Generations: v1/v2 boot+device chain (eb16281/145a428) → v3 DDA pipeline (0a4af03) → v4 (fdcf198) → v5 SetVolume full-call logger + GetVolume readbacks (fcd50d8) → round 7 unit-sound volume-chain probes (dc1d051, sandbox-local) → R8/R9 IEEE-bits + calc in/out probes (riding in fix 16's `9c8beb9`). Marks of record: `SetVolume#N: vol= glob= lin= att= buf= hr= from=`, `feed:`, `global volume -> N`, `stream OPEN`, `req:`, `updchan:`, `playsfx loadfail|nochannel|wavfail`, `fadein:`/`fadevol:`, `calc:`. With defect 13 closed and field-verified, **every `[sounddiag]` mark and its `support/d2_boot.h` include was stripped** (10 code files), plus the glide-era `[viddiag]` marks (Glide.cpp SetGamma ×2, D2GFX.cpp vidinit ×2) and Chat.cpp's dead include — 12 files, −140 lines; `rg '\[sounddiag\]|\[viddiag\]' -g '*.cpp' -g '*.h'` → 0. **`support/d2_boot.{h,cpp}` stays**: the permanent `[d2boot]` boot/crash marks (d2_crash.cpp, both Application.cpps, D2WinArchive/D2WinMain, Bnclient, ErrorManager/LogManager, glTextureCache, D2GFX, _unnamespaced) are load-bearing. If sound probes are ever needed again, the mark-log formats survive here and in the worklog — re-cut, don't reinvent.
- **DropDiag** hooks (Drop.cpp: include + `NoteRollLookup`/`NoteItemGen`/`NoteItemSpawnAudit`) — still in-tree, zero-behavior-change when idle.
- ShadowDiag/LightDiag/DropDiag are zero-behavior-change when idle and cheap (one file write per session each). Strip-or-env-gate them is an **offered, not requested** cleanup; if stripped, strip all hook lines *and* the modules, and keep handoff §8's grep gates accurate.

## H8. Delivery conventions — full historical text (current condensed rules live in handoff §6)

**The overlay model (glide era).** Deliverable = zip in `/home/z/my-project/download/`: `OVERLAY-PR2-FIXN.md` (root cause, evidence table, self-diagnosis greps, decision tree, branch matrix vanilla-plus vs pr2) + `patches/PATCH.diff` (normalized prefixes, minimal hunks) + `drop-in-pr2/` and `drop-in-vanilla-plus/` full files (fallback route). QA gauntlet (all green before shipping): `git apply --no-index` round-trip **byte-identical** on *every* supported base (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES=/home/z/my-project`); **negative test** — the patch must *refuse cleanly* on an already-fixed tree; unique-anchor asserts for every edit site on every base; new C++ files `g++ -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra` against stub headers; builders persisted as scripts.

**The fix-11 lesson, burned in:** the fix-11 zip's vanilla-plus drop-in was cut from the pre-fix-10 tip, so the user's whole-file copy reverted nine u32 stores and the light-radius discoloration returned in the next build (root-caused byte-level in Task 20; fixed by fix 12). Rules going forward: **patch-first** (full-file drop-ins fallback only); drop-ins must be cut **from the tip the user actually has** — verify with a base-state grep gate printed in the OVERLAY.md (e.g. `grep -c "glide fix 10" D2Glide/Renderer/Glide.cpp` → 0 = regressed, ≥1 = intact); deltas touching the same file must be QA'd order-independent.

**Git bundles (rev 4 route).** The sandbox has no push credentials, but whole commits transfer as bundles — history, authorship and fast-forward arrive intact:
```
git bundle create download/<repo>-vanilla-plus-<tip7>-bundle-<date>.bundle origin/vanilla-plus..vanilla-plus
# user side: git fetch <file>.bundle vanilla-plus && git merge --ff-only FETCH_HEAD && git push origin vanilla-plus
```
QA: `git bundle verify` in the sandbox clone AND in a scratch repo initialized at exactly the prerequisite tip; fetch+`--ff-only` must fast-forward cleanly, then content greps for every fix claimed; the prerequisite is the user's **pushed** tip — check `git rev-parse origin/vanilla-plus` AFTER a fresh `git fetch origin`, never from a stale remote-tracking ref (bit once: scratch was built at 9389e07 while the user was already at 0fa2003). Record bundle path + prerequisite + verification in the worklog.

**tmpfiles.org overlay zips (rev 5 route, the working loop since CI retirement).** Overlay zips of **only the files actually changed** — never a stale whole-file overlay (the user's tree carries their own commits; cut every delivery from a fresh `git fetch origin`). Zip layout: `<changed files at repo-relative paths>` + `MANIFEST.txt` (base commit, per-file sha256, base-state grep gates, apply/build steps, acceptance criteria). Upload to tmpfiles.org (**60-minute expiry**); the URL curl receives is an **HTML interstitial** — extract the tokenized `/dl/<token>` link embedded in that page, then **byte-verify** (`curl -o /tmp/f <dl-url> && cmp /tmp/f <local-file>`) and record the sha256. Post the `/dl/` link, never the interstitial URL.

**format-patch route (rev 7, used for upstream PR #10).** For PR-style deliveries: build the commits in a scratch worktree at the target base, `git format-patch <base> -o download/<dir>/`, `sha256sum *.patch > MANIFEST.sha256`, zip the directory, QA with `git apply --check` in sequence on a pristine checkout of the base, then diff -r the QA tree against the commit tree (must be identical).

## H9. Fullscreen D3D "Error 25" — gamma-control IID truncation (2026-10-28, fix 17)

Release 0.0.1 known issue "fullscreen Direct3D does not work" (handoff §3), generalized by field testing to every non-3dfx fullscreen mode. Diagnosis chain: the boot-trace build (`[d2boot]` marks) isolated the failure to `CreateWindowWithChecks -> 0` at renderer=6 (D3D) windowed=0; the retail "Error 25" MessageBox (raised by `D2GFX_Release`, gated by the `ghWndMain = 1` singlet at D2GFX.cpp:432 — the only assignment in the tree) proved the D3D provider's `nfpCreateWindow` returned 0; the FOG log (`Documents\Diablo II\logs\D2<YYMMDD>.txt`) named the exact step: `***** DirectDraw error: Failed to get gamma control interface! - DDERR_INVALIDPARAMS (0x80070057)` (Direct3D.cpp:1943). Root cause = §1 root pattern striking in .rdata: the Ghidra export kept only the first byte (0x3e) of the 16-byte IID_IDirectDrawGammaControl as `static uint8_t gszRendererD3DDeviceInitializationCode`; QueryInterface then received a garbage IID on the emulated DD7 primary surface (which DOES implement gamma control — the `gnD3DTextureCapFlags & 0x10` cap was legitimately set). Fix 17 restores the full IID {69C11C3E-B14B-11D1-9F93-00A0C90D32CB} as a proper GUID in globals/D2Direct3D.cpp + extern in globals.h; the other three GUIDs in that block verify correct against DX7 SDK values (only the gamma IID was truncated). Field notes from the same hunt: `-ddraw` is not a 1.13 switch (ini table has no entry → ignored → D3D default) and `-opengl` is a dead switch (no OPENGL branch in Application.cpp renderer selection; selector slot 5 is null) — both retail-faithful, only D3D + Glide are testable fullscreen. If a post-fix boot still fails, the NEXT `*****` line in the FOG log names the wall (GetGammaRamp / CreateDevice HAL / viewport chain — those would be genuine emulated-DD7 environment limits, i.e. retail parity, not rebuild defects).

## H10. Fullscreen D3D round 2 — gamma QI refused even with the correct IID; init degrades gracefully (2026-10-28, fix 18)

Field retest of fix 17 on the reporting box (`DESKTOP-43BT99S`): identical failure — `***** DirectDraw error: Failed to get gamma control interface! - DDERR_INVALIDPARAMS (0x80070057)` remains the ONLY `*****` line (FOG log 13:12:57 boot) and the boot-trace build still shows `CreateWindowWithChecks -> 0`. Since the fix-17 GUID bytes verify correct against the DX7 SDK value and the log site covers ONLY the QueryInterface (the following GetGammaRamp has its own message at :1960), the QI is genuinely being refused by the emulated DD7 stack on that box even with a valid IID — plausibly because the flip-chain front surface is created with caps FRONTBUFFER|FLIP|COMPLEX|3DDEVICE|VIDEOMEMORY (0x6218, no PRIMARYSURFACE bit) and modern ddraw emulation is strict about gamma control on non-primary flips; cannot be settled statically (whether the built binary actually contained fix 17 is also unverifiable remotely — both scenarios produce the same FOG line). Two facts made the response unambiguous: the `0x10` "gamma supported" bit comes from the registry (`Diablo II\VideoConfig` `dwFlags`, read at Direct3D.cpp:1846), not live driver enumeration; and every consumer of `g_pDDGammaControl` is ALREADY null-guarded — the brightness ramp path early-returns at :833, the release path skips ramp restore at :719. Fix 18 therefore makes both gamma init steps non-fatal: on QI or GetGammaRamp failure the FOG log gets `"... - continuing without hardware gamma"` and init proceeds with `g_pDDGammaControl = null` (the GetGammaRamp-failure branch releases the interface before nulling). This is a deliberate, documented deviation from retail (retail 1.13 aborts device init when the gamma interface is unavailable — i.e. retail would show its Error 25 on this box too); on systems where the QI succeeds, behavior is byte-identical to retail. Optional follow-up if the inert brightness slider annoys anyone: gate `CheckGamma()` (D2Client/Renderer/Direct3D.cpp:30) on `g_pDDGammaControl != 0` so the options slider hides instead of no-ops. No-rebuild field experiment that reproduces fix 18's gate with retail semantics: clear bit 0x10 in `HKCU\Software\Blizzard Entertainment\Diablo II\VideoConfig` `dwFlags` (note the original value first) — the game then skips the gamma QI entirely.

## H11. Fullscreen D3D round 3 — pixel-format template + adapter-GUID extent truncations (2026-10-28, fix 19)

Post-fix-18 boot got past the gamma wall and crashed ~550ms later: VEH `C0000005` read of `00000000` in `RENDERER_D3D_CreateFontTextures+0x219`, called via `RENDERER_D3D_InitializeFontSystem` ← `RENDERER_D3D_AllocateTextureCaches+0x1c7` (the perspective branch's `InitializeFontSystem(0)` at :666) ← `RENDERER_D3D_InitializeDevice+0x92d` (the AllocateTextureCaches call at :2046) ← provider `CreateWindow` ← `D2GFX_CreateWindow` ← `CreateWindowWithChecks` — i.e. inside the init window (no `CreateWindowWithChecks -> N` mark printed). Symbolized in the field against `build\d2exe.map` (which the build script emits every link) via a nearest-symbol-below PowerShell lookup; the fat `build\d2.exe` carries DWARF so `addr2line -f -C -i` works identically. Context: the user's box plays windowed via the GDI renderer and fullscreen via glide — this was the FIRST execution of the whole D3D texture-stack on any rebuild machine, so latent D3D export defects fire one at a time.

Root cause (§1 root pattern, `.data` extent flavor — third strike in this block after the fix-17 IID and the palette-adjacency family): the export typed `gsD3DTextureDescTemplate` as a single `uint32_t = 0x20` — which is only the DDPIXELFORMAT `dwSize` field — but BOTH texture-creation helpers copy EIGHT DWORDs from its address (`RENDERER_D3D_CreateSystemMemoryTexture` :908, `RENDERER_D3D_CreateSurfaceWithTexture` :2235). In the rebuild the trailing 7 DWORDs were whatever the linker placed after the 4-byte object → garbage pixel format → `CreateSurface` rejects it → every D3D texture creation fails → the FIRST consumer (font system) ignored the return (`CreateFontTextures` :3234; unlike `InitializeTexturePools` :3132-3160, retail never checks it there either) → `pMemorySurface == 0` → `Lock` through null at :3243 → read of 0 with EAX/ECX/EDX = 0 (classic `this->lpVtbl` fetch).

The correct template values are corroborated twice in-tree: (1) `D3D_EnumTextureFormatsCallback` (:87-102) — retail's own "supported texture format" predicate — compares the enumerated DDPIXELFORMAT at offsets +4/+0xC/+0x10/+0x14/+0x18/+0x1C against constants the decompiler mislabeled as "UnitSfx" statics: `0x41` (DDPF_RGB|DDPF_ALPHAPIXELS), `0x10` (16bpp), `0x7C00`/`0x3E0`/`0x1F` (masks), `0x8000` (alpha); (2) the `g_Palette16Bit` packer (D2Client/Renderer/Direct3D.cpp:51-87) emits three 5-bit channels with rounding plus a forced MSB = the same A1R5G5B5. Fix 19a restores `gsD3DTextureDescTemplate[8] = {0x20, 0x41, 0, 0x10, 0x7C00, 0x3E0, 0x1F, 0x8000}`.

Same-family latent defect fixed in the same pass (fix 19b): `gsD3DAdapterGuid` was exported as a 1-byte scalar, but `SRegLoadData("Diablo II\VideoConfig", "GUID", ..., &gsD3DAdapterGuid, 0x10, ...)` (:1853) writes 16 bytes into it every D3D boot and `DirectDrawCreate` consumes it as a full GUID (:2280, when dwFlags bit0 is clear). The spill overwrote the adjacent BSS globals `GlobalGreen`/`GlobalBlue`/`GlobalRed` — the D3D vertex-lighting tint state (:4534-4540, indexes `g_aLightingTable` for vertex diffuse colors) — a latent D3D lighting-corruption bug. Restored as `uint8_t gsD3DAdapterGuid[16]`.

Fix 19 rev 2 (the field build broke): the 19b extent change itself broke the compile — `Initialize` (:1853) passes `&gsD3DAdapterGuid` to `SRegLoadData`'s `LPBYTE` parameter; with the old 1-byte scalar `&scalar` was `uint8_t*`, with the array it is `uint8_t(*)[16]`, which has no implicit conversion (`error: cannot convert 'uint8_t (*)[16]' to 'LPBYTE' {aka 'unsigned char*'}`). Fix 19c casts the decayed array pointer explicitly — `(LPBYTE)gsD3DAdapterGuid`, same address, same 16 bytes. Audit of every 19a/19b use site confirmed the other three (`:908`, `:2235`, `:2280`) already used explicit `&`-casts and were unaffected; no other TU references either symbol; no `sizeof()` anywhere. Same pass, fix 19d closed the sibling under-extent: `Initialize`'s static `gD3DAdapterInfoBuffer` was a 1-byte export remnant, but the `SRegLoadString(..., "DeviceName", ..., 0x100)` at :1844 caps the write at 0x100 bytes and retail itself writes `VideoConfig\DeviceName` (`SaveDeviceConfigToRegistry`, :585-606) — so from the second D3D boot onward the load overran the byte by up to 255 bytes. The string is only an existence probe (the buffer is never read back), but the write was real; restored `uint8_t gD3DAdapterInfoBuffer[0x100]`. Lesson for the extent pattern going forward: **resizing an exported global is not compile-neutral — re-grep every `&symbol` use site and re-check the parameter types it feeds.**

Ledger notes from the same read: the exported `IID_IDirectDraw7` bytes are actually the **IDirectDraw4** IID (`9C59509A-...`), and `D3DGuid` is **IID_IDirect3D3** (`BB223240-...`) — both are retail's own `.rdata` bytes and were empirically validated by the field box's boot progressing through DirectDrawCreate, both QIs, SetDisplayMode, CreateSurface, and CreateDevice — do NOT "correct" them. `RENDERER_D3D_CopySurfacePixels` (:958-993): the round-3 recon initially flagged its `memcpy(pSrcRowCopy, pDestRowCopy, ...)` as transposed; full caller analysis shows the data flows texture←memory correctly (the internal parameter naming is simply inverted) — only its two lock-failure log strings are swapped, cosmetic, no fix. Expected post-fix behavior: font textures create, cache inits proceed, `ClearBackbuffer`+`Flip` run, boot continues; any further wall names itself in the FOG log `*****` lines (or a new d2crash — symbolize the same way).

## H12. Fullscreen D3D round 4 — intro plays, then HALT 0xd7: phantom sprite-cache structs (2026-10-28, fix 20)

Post-fix-19-rev-2 boot was the breakthrough round: `CreateWindowWithChecks -> 1` (the Error-25 wall that started the whole hunt, retired), provider init/InitPerspective/pre-setup all `-> 1`, and the intro video **plays with music** in fullscreen D3D. Then ~5.1s after mode-loop entry: clean `HALT line=215 (0xd7)` at addr 0116E8F3, followed by a secondary VEH 0xC0000005 during error-path cleanup (expected to vanish with the halt fixed — the halt path tears down a half-initialized device; if a halt-cleanup AV ever recurs, take it from the d2crash stack).

The decode took one grep: the only `0xd7` halt in the tree is `D3DTEXCACHE_AllocateNodeFromFreeList` (d3dTextureCache.cpp:101) — free list empty (`pFreeHead == 0`) while `nUsedCount != nMaxNodes`, an invariant-violation halt. Analysis of the module: `D3DTEXCACHE_EnsureAvailableNode`'s eviction loop cannot rescue a never-seeded cache (nUsedCount 0 skips eviction → retry hits the same empty free list), and a *negative* `nMaxNodes` (bad memory math) was eliminated because the seed's `D2_ALLOC`/`memset` would have AV'd at boot, not halted at 5s. The only remaining state was "cache struct never seeded at all".

That state is real because of a **new §1 variant — phantom function-statics**: `RENDERER_DIRECT3D_UploadTextureToCache` (Direct3D.cpp:2426) selected its cache with retail-image arithmetic — `pCache = (D3DTextureCacheStrc*)(&DAT_00970eac + class*0x1c)` and node bases via `(&INT_00970ec4)[class*7]` — where `DAT_00970eac`/`INT_00970ec4` were the export's 1-byte/4-byte remnants of the three contiguous `D3DTextureCacheStrc`s (0x1c bytes each) that live in the rebuild as `gD3DGenericTextureCache.aCaches[0..2]` (globals/D2Direct3D.cpp:2268), seeded by `RENDERER_D3D_InitializeTextureCachePool`. The upload therefore read an unseeded BSS struct: garbage `nMaxNodes`, `nUsedCount` 0, `pFreeHead` null → halt 0xd7 on the **first DC6-sprite upload** — which is exactly the first-logo boundary of the intro (Smacker frames don't use this path, which is why the video itself played).

The corrected mapping (class 1/2/3 → `aCaches[0/1/2]`, textures 0x100/0x80/0x20) is corroborated three ways: the pool sizes match the three `CreateSystemMemoryTexture` calls (`InitializeTexturePools` :3142-3152); the `D3D_PixelDataFreeCallback_ClearCacheNodes` callback (:2369-2384) already addresses the real caches with the same `aCaches[class-1]` / `pNodeBuffer[idx-1]` indexing; and the class thresholds (:2457-2466: ≤0x20→3, ≤0x80→2, ≤0x100→1) match the pool texture sizes. Fix 20 replaces the phantom arithmetic with `pCache = &gD3DGenericTextureCache.aCaches[iMipLevel - 1]` + a `pCacheNodeBase = pCache->pNodeBuffer` local at all four `INT_00970ec4` sites, and deletes the two phantom statics. A repo-wide sweep for `DAT_/INT_/UNK_` label arithmetic found exactly one other suspect — `Storm/_unnamespaced.cpp:789` `INT_007a6b0c = (int)(1000 / (int64_t)gaKeyBindingDefaultArray)` (dividing by an array *address* is surely a mislabeled scalar) — logged as an audit item, untouched.

Footnotes from the same read: (1) `apSpriteTextures[class+2]` (:2484) formally indexes past the 3-element array into `apSpriteSurfaces[0..2]` — correct-by-adjacency (retail's flat layout; sizes line up 0x100/0x80/0x20), fragile if the struct field order ever changes — audit note only. (2) If `GetAvailableVidMem` fails on the emulated DD7, its failures appear in the FOG log as `Failed to get available texture/local texture memory` `*****` lines and poison the pool sizing (`uSmallCacheAlloc = g_nVideoMemoryTotal`, :624/:2027) — check the FOG log if further cache halts appear. (3) This upload function alone carries 11 distinct halt codes (0x164/0x165/0x16d/0x16e/0x171/0x172/0x19a/0x19b/0x1b4/0x1b5/0x1c0) — a future halt here names its own branch. Lesson added to the §1 pattern list: **phantom function-statics (`DAT_`/`INT_` labels + raw `&label + k*sizeof` arithmetic) are extent truncations in disguise — grep for the label pattern, not just for `sizeof` mismatches.**


## H13 — Fix 21: `HALT line=365 (0x16d)` at the first sprite upload — the D3D upload path never received the glide series' sprite-header slot guards (2026-10-27, round 8)

Field: user's local build `f50099f` (fixes 17+18+19rev2+20 applied; "local builds don't always rotate commit hash" — the hash is their local commit). Fullscreen D3D boot: new clean halt `HALT line=365 (0x16d)` at `d2s-f50099f.exe+0x234188`, fault frame `+0x44840F` (Fog halt manager). Single-grep decode (round-7 precedent + the H12 footnote that predicted this): the only `0x16d` halt in the tree is `RENDERER_DIRECT3D_UploadTextureToCache` (Direct3D.cpp:2521 pre-fix-21) — **the halt code names the branch**: a freshly-acquired cache node's `pSurface` (+0x10) is null.

The round-8 analysis is a study in how far a contradiction can be pushed before the data decides. Exhaustive static audit of the D3D cache machinery found every path consistent: the seeder `RENDERER_D3D_InitializeTextureCachePool` (:5039) gives every node 0..nMaxNodes-1 a surface+texture pair via `CreateSurfaceWithTexture` (node-0 failure = halt 0x5a at boot — didn't happen; later failure truncates `nMaxNodes` coherently and zeroes the chain tail); `AllocateNodeFromFreeList`/`EnsureAvailableNode`/`InvalidateByIndex`/`InsertNodeAtTail` bookkeeping verified sound; `gD3DGenericTextureCache` zero-initialized; the per-frame descriptor blocks (0x2c bytes per DC6 frame, allocated+zeroed by `CELCMP_FixupPointersAndPrepare` :1464-1478, `[class, nodeIdx]` slot pairs at `mode*8`) legitimate retail design with all writers inside bounds; upload callers pass only modes 1/2/3/4; stack frame bands (via the round-5 map mapping) place the halt in the normal post-intro render loop, i.e. the FIRST sprite upload of the session on freshly seeded caches. Under those invariants halt 0x16d is unreachable — the state it reports cannot exist for a *seeded cache addressed with a sane class*.

The resolution: **the class is not sane.** The decisive cross-reference was in our own tree all along — `Glide.cpp:1053-1059`, the field-verified **glide fix 3** from the closed glide series: "Sprite headers come from a non-zeroing pool, so a first-use header can carry pool garbage in the size-class slot. Treat an out-of-range class like an unset one (recompute below) and zero the slot so the recompute sticks." The D3D upload path tests the class slot for **exact zero only** (Direct3D.cpp:2469 pre-fix), so a garbage slot value outside {0,1,2,3} skips the size recompute and flows into `pCache = &gD3DGenericTextureCache.aCaches[class - 1]` — **past the 3-entry array**. `D3DTextureCacheSetStrc` is packed: `aCaches[3]` aliases `apSpriteTextures[0..1]` (offset 0x54), `aCaches[4]` hits `apSpriteSurfaces` and beyond — the adjacency lottery. A pointer-shaped `pFreeHead` there is non-null (no halt 0xd7), `nUsedCount != nMaxNodes` as two different pointers-as-ints (no early return), and the "popped node" is aliased memory whose +0x10 reads zero — exactly halt 0x16d, cleanly, at the first upload with a garbage header. This is the D3D twin of glide defects 2/3/4 (the stale/garbage sprite-header slot family, ledger rows 2-4) — never ported because **the D3D upload path had never executed until this week**.

FIX 21 (Direct3D.cpp only, both guards inside `RENDERER_DIRECT3D_UploadTextureToCache`):
1. **Glide fix-3 mirror** (before the class recompute): `if (slot < 1 || 3 < slot) slot = 0;` — out-of-range class (including garbage and negatives) is treated as unset so the width/height recompute pins the class to 1..3.
2. **Glide fix-2 mirror** (after `pCacheNodeBase`, before the reuse deref): if the header's node-index slot is non-zero, validate it against the pool bound (`(uint32_t)(idx-1) < nMaxNodes` — hardened beyond glide, which dereferenced unbounded) and the node's own backpointers (+4 block ptr, +8 mip idx — both written by this very function, so a legit palette-change re-upload matches by construction); on mismatch drop the slot to 0 and allocate fresh.

Residuals/footnotes from the same read: (a) `D3D_PixelDataFreeCallback_ClearCacheNodes` (:2367-2385) acts on all five slot pairs with only a signed `>= 1` guard — a never-uploaded frame with garbage [class>=1, idx>=1] slots could still spuriously clear a node's tile pointer (or worse) at sprite-free time; glide's field-verified fix 2 left the identical residual in `GLIDE_PixelDataFreeCallback_ClearCacheNodes`, so it is mirrored as-is and logged as an audit item, not fixed. (b) The DCC combined-blob loader (`CelCmp.cpp:1398`) `D2_ALLOC`s without memset — a corroborating non-zeroing sprite-container route. (c) H12's footnote-1 adjacency note (`apSpriteTextures[class+2]` -> `apSpriteSurfaces`) is the same packed-struct layout this halt exploited maliciously at `aCaches[3+]`; correct-by-adjacency at class <=3, hostile beyond.

Institutional lesson added to the §1 pattern list: **when a code path first executes, audit it against the fixes its sibling paths already received.** The glide series closed 14 fixes deep; the D3D renderer shares the sprite-header infrastructure and inherited none of that hardening. A one-line grep ("what does the working renderer do at this exact site?") answered in seconds what hours of single-module static analysis could not — the module-internal proofs were all correct; the garbage was coming from *outside the module's invariants*.

## H14 — Fix 22: the 0x16d wall recurs through an applied fix — verify what the fix actually gates, close the proven WRITE side, and make the halt site a measurement point (2026-10-27, round 9)

Field: user's build `d2s-0604b96` (hash rotated from `f50099f` — fix 21 confirmed in; the halt site shifted +0x99, exactly the size of the added guard code, which is itself positive identification) boots identically — intro plays, mode loop entered — and clean-halts `HALT line=365 (0x16d)` at the same first-sprite-upload spot. **A fix whose target halt recurs unchanged must be treated as a falsified theory, not bad luck.** So round 9 re-verified every premise of H13's residual theory *by direct reading, this round, no reuse of round-8 conclusions*:

- Seeder `RENDERER_D3D_InitializeTextureCachePool` (:5070-5123): every free-list node gets a surface+texture; mid-seed failure truncates `nMaxNodes` AND zeroes `pPrev->pNext` coherently — no unseeded node is reachable. The "negative node count" scenario self-checks: it would AV at boot (memset through null/huge alloc), which did not happen.
- `RENDERER_D3D_CreateSurfaceWithTexture` (:2238): DD_OK ⇒ non-null surface out; QI fail ⇒ Release+FALSE. TRUE with a null out is impossible.
- `AllocateNodeFromFreeList`/`EnsureAvailableNode`/`InvalidateByIndex`/`InsertNodeAtTail`: alloc/evict/invalidate never clear node +0x10; eviction clears the owning descriptor's slot pair via the node's tile backpointer.
- Layouts: `d3dTextureCache.h` is the single definition (globals.h includes it); the packed `D3DTextureCacheSetStrc` adjacency (`apSpriteTextures[class+2]` → `apSpriteSurfaces`) is correct-by-adjacency at class 1..3.
- Descriptor truth: `CELCMP_GetDC6BlockPixelData` returns `*(block+0x18)` = the 0x2c descriptor base written by `FixupPointersAndPrepare` (memset); slots live INSIDE it; `DeleteCellFile` invokes the free callback with that same base per frame.

Verdict: **post-fix-21, halt 0x16d is unreachable through any read path.** The state it reports can only be produced by a write the read-side guards cannot see. The family's one proven unbounded write is the round-8 audit-noted residual: `D3D_PixelDataFreeCallback_ClearCacheNodes` validated each `[class, idx]` pair with only a signed `>= 1` and wrote `aCaches[class-1].pNodeBuffer[idx-1].pAssociatedTilePtr = 0` — no class range check (class > 3 indexes past `aCaches[0..2]`), no idx upper bound (unbounded wild write past the node buffer), no owner match (a stale/garbage pair cleared a foreign node's backpointer). Feeding it garbage: pairs 0 and 4 are NEVER written by the upload path, and DCC combined-blob frames (`CELCMP_SerializeCelFileToMemory`, `D2_ALLOC` without memset) carry 0x23-byte headers — 5-pair reads at +0x20 overrun into structural/pixel bytes (H13 footnote b upgraded from "corroborating" to "load-bearing"). The stride math is exact: the write lands at `base + (idx-1)*0x20 + 4`, so it can never directly zero a node's +0x10 — but out-of-range `idx` corrupts arbitrary heap, and heap corruption happily zeroes whatever the next upload pops. Cannot be proven offline; it is now closed regardless.

FIX 22 (three parts, one file + one banner):
1. **Free-callback hardening** (`D3DTEXCACHE_ClearCacheNodeSlot` + 5 validated calls): range-check class 1..3 and idx 1..nMaxNodes against the real pool, then require the node's own +4 backpointer to equal the descriptor being freed (that field is written by `UploadTextureToCache` and nothing else, so every legit free matches by construction). Legit frees behave byte-identically to retail; garbage pairs are skipped; the FIRST skipped pair of each dangerous shape (out-of-range class, out-of-bounds idx, foreign node) is reported to d2boot.log via `D2BOOT_MarkFmt` — if the field ever shows `fix22: free-callback skipped ... (was a wild write)`, the garbage-pair theory was live, not theoretical. Glide's twin callback stays as delivered (field-verified working renderer — asymmetric hardening is deliberate and noted).
2. **Halt-site self-diagnostics** (`RENDERER_D3D_UploadHaltDump` before all 12 halt sites in `UploadTextureToCache`): one d2boot.log line per site with mode/mip/class/node/sprite dims, one with the pool's identity and bookkeeping (`pool= max= used= freeHead= freeTail= usedHead= usedTail= nodes=`), one with the failing node's full contents when in-bounds, one with the class's permanent sprite texture/surface pointers. The next field round names its failing premise from the log alone — no more blind fix rounds at this wall.
3. **Fixset boot banner** (`vanilla-plus fixset through fix 22`, Application.cpp): field logs self-identify the fix level regardless of commit-hash rotation — the "local builds don't always rotate the hash" confusion cost this project one full round of analysis ambiguity.

Institutional lessons: **(a) when a fix's target halt recurs through an applied fix, re-derive every premise from the current source instead of trusting the previous round's audit — round 9 falsified H13's surviving theory and found the real remaining hole (write side) in the same pass; (b) a halt site is a measurement point, not just a failure — instrument the state around it BEFORE the next field round so the log decides between the surviving theories; (c) builds must self-identify their fix level in their own boot log.**

## H15 — Fix 23: the fix-22 dump catches the real root cause — C pointer-scaling on the D3D upload's typed node pointer: the "null surface" halt read 0x3E0 bytes past the node buffer while the real node was fully seeded (2026-10-27, round 10)

Field: post-fix-22 build (fixset banner present), the `upload-halt 0x16d-surface` dump fired for the first time and decided the round from the log alone:
- pool line: `max=178 used=7 freeHead=110820E0 freeTail=11083620 usedHead=11082000 usedTail=110820C0 nodes=11082000` — freeTail−nodes = 177×0x20 exactly for max=178, freeHead = usedTail+0x20, node stride 0x20 exact ⇒ pool identity, seeding, and bookkeeping all sane;
- node line: `node[7] pos=6 tile=05AE8508 mip=1 pal=00000000 surf=0E0B8A18 tex=0FA87324 next=0 prev=110820A0` — tile/mip were written by this very call, surf/tex are the seeder's, prev/next are a coherent used-tail ⇒ the "null surface" halt fired on a node whose +0x10 was NEVER null;
- spriteTex line: class-1 texture+surface slots populated.

A halt that tests node+0x10==0 cannot fire while the same node's +0x10 prints non-null ⇒ the check and the dump read DIFFERENT addresses. The dump reads `pCache->pNodeBuffer[nNodeIdx-1]` (C-indexed, byte-exact); the halt check added the raw byte offset (idx*0x20−0x10) to a `D3DTextureCacheNodeStrc*` — and C scales `ptr + int` by sizeof = 0x20, so the true address was base+(idx*0x20−0x10)×0x20: idx 1..6 land inside the seeded buffer on other nodes' `nIndex` (non-zero ⇒ six silent false-passes), idx=7 lands at base+0x1A00 = 0x3E0 bytes PAST the 178×0x20=0x1620 node buffer ⇒ heap zero ⇒ HALT 0x16d. The field log's `used=7, node=7` is the arithmetic's own prediction — the defect selects its own halt round.

Same disease, two more sites in the same function: the reuse-path stored-palette compare (offset idx*0x20−0x14 scaled ⇒ never matched ⇒ cache reuse never fired) and the SetTexture source read (offset idx*0x20−0xC scaled ⇒ would hand IDirect3DDevice7::SetTexture a garbage pointer on the first mode-4 re-upload — an in-game AV waiting to happen). Glide compiles the identical transcription BYTE-EXACT because `D2Client/Renderer/Glide.h:36` types `pNodes` as `uint8_t*`, while `d3dTextureCache.h` types the base as the node struct — one header typing difference is why -3dfx survived the same decompiler output and D3D never cleared the first big sprite. This retroactively explains round 9 (halt at the 7th class-1 allocation, no dump yet to show it) and demotes the fix-22 wild-write theory to a hardened-but-unproven sibling (kept: defense in depth, byte-identical for legit frees).

FIX 23: all three raw-offset sites rewritten to the byte-exact form the fix-21 validation already uses — `(uint8_t*)pCacheNodeBase + (*piVar2 - 1) * 0x20 + OFF` (OFF = 0xC stored palette / 0x10 pSurface / 0x14 pTexture). Boot banner → "vanilla-plus fixset through fix 23". The fix-22 dump infrastructure stays unchanged — it earned its keep by converting a third blind round into a one-line diagnosis.

Institutional lessons: **(a) pointer-scaling lens — in decompiled rebuilds, audit every `typed_ptr + raw_byte_offset` expression for implicit sizeof scaling; the C type system quietly multiplies raw offsets by the pointee size, and the correct raw-offset idiom is `(uint8_t*)ptr + offset` or C-indexed `&arr[i].field`; (b) the sibling-renderer contrast is the tell — when the same transcription works in one renderer and halts in another, diff the HEADER TYPING of the shared pattern before doubting the logic; (c) a self-contradicting measurement beats a contradiction audit — rounds 8–9 "proved" the halt unreachable by reading the source as byte-exact, while one dump line pair (healthy surf + fired surf-halt) falsified the shared premise in seconds; (d) QA-gate discipline, 4th occurrence — fix comments must describe removed forms, never quote them verbatim, or the old-form grep gate self-matches.**

## H16 — Fix 24: the first playable fullscreen-D3D round — black ground = three phantom index-buffer statics; "Expansion Character" purple = the gnPaletteIndexCounter extent clobber finally field-confirmed (2026-10-27, round 8/round 11)

Field: post-fix-23 build is PLAYABLE fullscreen D3D (title, char select, in-game all reached — the 0x16d wall is dead). Two color defects reported: (1) in-game ground renders ALL BLACK (fullscreen D3D; windowed normal); (2) char-select "Expansion Character" renders purple instead of green.

GROUND (fix 24a). Elimination chain: g_Palette16Bit is shared by the sprite conversion (`RENDERER_D3D_ConvertPalettedTo16Bit`) and the floor pixel fill (`D3D_RenderTileComponentsToPixelBuffer`) — sprites are colored, so the palette table and floor TEXTURES are fine; `g_aLightingTable` is both statically initialized and runtime-initialized (:2325); the light-diffuse data is shared with the GDI renderer (correct there). That left the draw primitive itself: `DrawGroundTile` is the D3D provider's ONLY `DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB` call site (walls use the subtile/DrawPrimitive path — why walls survived), and its index source was `(uint32_t*)&gpD3DGroundTileVertexBuffer` — a 1-BYTE phantom static — while `RENDERER_D3D_InitGroundTileCache` built 65 index WORDs into `gaD3DGroundTileIndexBuffer` — a 2-BYTE phantom static (129-byte overrun). Two decompiler labels for one retail buffer, materialized as two distinct function-local statics by the transpile: prefill filled one, the draw read the other, `DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB` consumed garbage WORDs and drew nothing — black ground, sprites unaffected (DrawPrimitiveVB takes no index list), GDI unaffected (no VB path). Third phantom: `gdwRendererMode = 0x50000` — its value IS the low-quality fan list's first dword (WORDs [0,5]); the draw read 4 WORDs from it, so words [2,3] were adjacent-static garbage. Fix: real shared arrays — `g_aD3DGroundTileStripIndices[65]` (5 bands × (12 pair words [c, c+6] + 1 trailing duplicate = the odd-parity band join; declared types `uint16_t* puDestIndex` + `short local_9c[60]` confirm the 13-word band geometry)) and `g_aD3DGroundTileFanIndices[4] = {0,5,35,28}` ([0,5] verbatim from the retail dword; [35,28] = the two remaining per-frame-updated vertices, diamond-perimeter fan order).

"EXPANSION CHARACTER" (fix 24b). This is the Task-2/3 glide "discolored text items" sighting with a repro: `gnPaletteIndexCounter` was declared `uint32_t` (4B) but `D2WINPAL_LoadPaletteFiles` writes 40B there (9 PALETTEENTRY + 1 manual triple) — 36B overran into `gPaletteAct1[0..35]` (gcc adjacency), overwriting act1 plane-0 remap entries 0-8 with text-color RGB bytes read as remap indices; any OOG/act1 sprite resolving through those entries tints wrong. Glide never reads table[0..2] (Task 3) — but glide's OWN DrawImage path receives the same act1 remap POINTERS from shared UI callers, so both renderers are exposed; D3D's colored-text multiply path (0x45 triples) is exonerated for this symptom by arithmetic: a green diffuse multiply can never produce purple. Declared `uint8_t gnPaletteIndexCounter[40]` (consumers read the same verbatim bytes via table slot 0x45). If purple persists past this fix, the documented next lens is the D3D glyph-texel mapping (glide format-5 palette-free texel vs D3D `g_Palette16Bit[rawidx]`) — the D3D text code is retail-identical, so a residual would be data/state, not logic.

Institutional lessons: **(a) an index buffer is data, not plumbing — when a draw renders NOTHING while its texture/diffuse inputs verify clean, suspect the index list before the pixel pipeline; (b) phantom-static triads (a buffer split into under-extent labels across decl/write/read sites) are the recurring §1 variant — the fix-20/22/24 family all reduce to "one retail object, several rebuild labels"; grep the write site AND the read site for label agreement every time a buffer is audited; (c) a color defect with a hue ROTATION (green→purple) is arithmetic evidence: multiply-by-diffuse pipelines cannot rotate hue, so the fault is upstream of the multiply (data, remap, or texture), never in the blend state.**

## H17 — The ad964f3 merge: upstream's re-typed tree supersedes 6 of the 17 D3D/glide hand fixes; fix-24b's extent corrected 40→39 by rereading the loader (2026-10-28, merge round)

Upstream rewrote `eef5f2c` as `3f6da07` (tree-identical — the "new commit" was metadata-only) and landed **`ad964f3`** on top (450 files, +181k: overlay-crash fixes, Esc speech boxes, unnamed data tables restored, D2_GLOBAL_GUARD/D2_STATIC_GUARD guard zones, `__frame0_t` frame groups). Merge `8030526` on vanilla-plus. The decisive trick: because our tree was exactly `eef5f2c` + our fixes and theirs exactly `eef5f2c` + `ad964f3`, every hand merge ran `git merge-file` with **`eef5f2c` as the base** — the 85 conflicts marked precisely where our fixes meet upstream's re-typing, with zero noise from the already-absorbed `eef5f2c` content.

What upstream had fixed in the meantime (supersessions): fix 17 (the gamma IID is now the head of a 272-byte init-data blob), fix 19a/b/d (template `[8]` with our exact A1R5G5B5 values, `GUID` type, `[264]` DeviceName buffer), fix 20 (the upload now addresses the real pools via `(cache − 0x1c + class·0x1c)`), fix 23 (**the big one**: upstream computes node addresses in INT arithmetic — `iCacheNodeIdx − 0x10 + base` — byte-exact by construction, so the typed-pointer-scaling defect class cannot exist there), glide defects 3/7/8 (`EnsureCapacity` returns typed), and fix 24b's extent. What upstream still lacked (re-ported by `scripts/port_d3d_fixes.py`): fix 18 (their gamma path is still retail-fatal), fix 21 (they still trust the class slot and the stale node index), fix 22 (halt sites un-instrumented, free callback still only signed-checks), fix 24a (the ground-tile phantom triad persists, now guard-wrapped).

The 39-vs-40 lesson: our fix-24b field analysis said `gnPaletteIndexCounter` extent = 40 B (9 PALETTEENTRY + "1 manual triple (4B)"); rereading the loader for the merge showed the manual write touches peRed/peGreen/peBlue only — **the peFlags 4th byte is never written, so the true extent is 39 B**, exactly what upstream declared (with a guard zone after it). Our `[40]` was safe (1 slack byte, consumers read bytes 0..38) but wrong as documentation — a reminder that "extent = what the writer writes" must count BYTES at the write site, not round up to the struct stride.

Institutional lessons: **(a) when a remote rewrites a commit you already merged, diff the rewrite against the old tip FIRST — a tree-identical rewrite converts a terrifying-looking 85-conflict merge into "ad964f3 only", and supplies the perfect merge base (`eef5f2c`) for every hand file; (b) never merge a re-typed tree by trusting textual auto-merges in the files you own fixes in — the first auto-resolution of `Direct3D.cpp` produced a working-looking chimera (our array wired at one site, upstream's phantom still live at two others); the durable pattern is the 45c6c2d one: take upstream's file wholesale, re-port each still-needed fix as an anchored edit, and gate every anchor count; (c) supersession is asymmetric — fix what upstream lacks (18/21/22/24a), retire what it fixed (17/19/20/23) with an explicit "do not re-add" note, and record both directions or the next round will re-fight them.**

## H18 — The fullscreen-D3D recovery endgame: a merge-spliced band loop, three probe rounds, and the renderer that skipped a palette family (2026-10-28, rounds 9–12, fixes 25–27)

Fix 25: the first post-merge field round found evenly spaced diagonal black bars over the perspective floor (title fine). Root cause: the ad964f3 merge spliced upstream's decompiled band loop — written against the old phantom anchor, which sat 12 words INTO the strip-index table — onto fix 24's base-pointer init, so bands 1–5 wrote words [−12..52] and the trailing bands consumed garbage indices in `DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB`. Fix restored the fix-24 fill of words [0..64] from the array base (`RENDERER_D3D_InitGroundTileCache`, `f13a5a8`). Lesson: after a wholesale-file re-port (H17 discipline (b)), re-gate the ARRAY-WRITING loops, not just the declarations — fix 24's arrays were present, correctly declared and correctly read; only the loop that filled them had been silently re-pointed.

Fixes 26A/B/C (probe rounds, `e9b4223`/`c469364`/folded into `00d97a0`): three one-shot diagnostic rounds against "Copyright renders teal / Expansion Character renders purple". A proved the 13 canonical UI colors load correctly (the palettes are innocent). B's bake-stamp + caller-RA + solid-branch dump proved the wrong hue was baked into glyph TEXELS at first upload — a purple output under a green diffuse multiply is arithmetically impossible, so the slot-0x45 path was exonerated — and captured the fechar↔sky palette ping-pong (~16 ms apart, repeating 1–2 s) on the char-select screen. C's per-frame bake key + font-ramp sentinels pinned the mechanism: glyphs first-bake under whatever LUT family is loaded at that instant, and the D3D text cache never re-bakes or flushes on palette change.

Fix 27 (root cause, `00d97a0`): **D3D was the ONLY renderer skipping the 13 pl2 UI-transpose palettes.** GDI/DDraw remap glyph pixels through `gpPaletteTable[shift+0x34]` per draw; glide stores palette-free texels and re-downloads its color table per palette; D3D baked raw glyph indices through `g_Palette16Bit` ONCE (pPalette=nullptr) and leaned on the 0x45 diffuse multiply, which only works for white-ramp glyph bands — family-dependent bands rendered teal/purple under sky/act1 and the frozen cache locked it in. Fix: bake shifted glyphs through the family LUT and draw the quad solid (GDI parity), flush transpose-range cache nodes on LUT-family change, and restore fechar's retail flag semantics (a fechar load no longer re-arms the FORMS_DestroyLists reload). Field-confirmed: "Colors are correct in D3D fullscreen now!"

Institutional lessons: **(a) a renderer-specific defect can hide behind "this code path is retail-identical" — the D3D text path was transcribed correctly and still rendered wrong, because the defect was an OMISSION (a palette family the other two renderers consumed and D3D never did); when one of N implementations misbehaves, diff what the others DO against it, not just what it does wrong; (b) cheap one-shot probes that discriminate between candidate stages out-derive theory — each A/B/C round killed exactly one candidate mechanism.**

## H19 — The first opt-in feature and 0.0.2: alt-key item-label toggle + self-provisioning config (2026-10-28)

The feature (local `ce02363`, upstream `dec7a22`): Alt toggles ground-item labels on/off — D2R "Show Items: Toggle" parity — gated behind `[features] alt_toggle=1` in `d2vp.ini`, default OFF = vanilla hold-Alt byte-for-byte (charter Principle 1). Design facts worth keeping (handoff ledger row 28): the 0x1c registration fires `HandleMouseButtonEvent`'s non-zero-event branch on clicks AND app-activation — vanilla uses it to clear labels while holding Alt; in toggle mode every flag writer converges to the latch, which is also what heals a stuck Alt after a lost keyup across alt-tab. Repeat-safe by construction: CmdTbl filters lParam bit-30 auto-repeat upstream, so keydown fires exactly once per physical press. The feature belongs in the game source because the wrapper can only inject OS-level input — toggle semantics need the state where the flag lives.

The addendum (local `c7e7faa`, upstream `9cf9f69`): a missing `d2vp.ini` is auto-created with a documented all-defaults template — one attempt per process (boot hook in `ApplicationMain` + lazy hook before the first config read), `CREATE_NEW` after an existence check so a user's edited file can never be clobbered, silent-fail so config-absence stays the vanilla path.

Shipping: the local chain was pushed rebased as `dec7a22`+`9cf9f69` on top of `00d97a0` (fix 27); release **`D2LoD-Vanilla-Plus-0.0.2`** published from `9cf9f69` — the 7z bundles `d2s-9cf9f69.exe` + `glide3x.dll` (Glide3xVK 0.4.5) + launcher/debug scripts. CI (mingw-i686) was re-enabled and ran green on the tag — the H2 retirement ended. Known packaging debt: the 7z's two script names are swapped (`Launch.cmd` installs the shader pack; `install_shaders.cmd` launches the game) — fix before the next release. Lessons: **(a) converge every writer of a UI flag onto one source of truth (the latch) instead of hunting writers one by one; (b) a config file the user can discover beats documentation — auto-create it, never overwrite it, and keep failures silent; (c) the sandbox resets between sessions — the release-notes markdown and delivery zips in `/home/z/my-project/download/` are ephemeral, the repo docs (this file, handoff.md) are the durable record.**

*Rev 11 (2026-10-28, portable-save feature): **second opt-in feature shipped**: `[features] portable_save=1` in `d2vp.ini` pins single-player saves to `<game folder>\save` (exe-anchored, folder auto-created) instead of the registry-resolved save path — motivated by the user's machine, where a stale registry save-path value from an old install pins saves to `F:\Diablo II\Save`. One hook at the top of `GetAndCreateSavePath` (Fog/Source/SFile.cpp — the choke point ALL 16 consumer call sites share) + the documented `portable_save=0` block in the auto-created ini template (ALTTOGGLE_WriteIniTemplate). Flag polled at the same ~2 s GetTickCount cadence as alt_toggle; exe dir resolved once via SFILE_GetExecutableDirectory; registry untouched in portable mode (no migration pass); missing ini/key/garbage = retail byte-for-byte. *Rev 12 (2026-10-28, alt-toggle click-through fix): **field bug in the alt-toggle feature fixed**: with labels latched ON (`alt_toggle=1`), left-clicks stopped attacking/interacting — every click turned into a walk. Root cause: while `UI_HOLDALT` is set, vanilla's ground-label renderer `DRAW_AltItems` (UI/ShowItems.cpp, runs every frame) OWNS the click selection — it calls `GAME::Select::ClearSelectedUnit()` every frame the cursor is over no label rect, plus a mid-loop branch revokes a selected item whose label is not under the cursor; and `HitTestScreen`'s UNIT_ITEM case excludes ground items from hit-testing while the flag is set, which also nullifies the renderer's own over-label select (`SELECT_UpdateUnitSelection` routes through `HitTestScreen`). Attacks/interactions need `GetSelectedUnit()` to return a target (`ClickMap` case 0 walks when NULL), so latched labels = selection wiped every frame = clicks only ever walk. Vanilla hold-Alt masks it (transient hold); the latch makes it permanent. Fix: new predicate `ALTTOGGLE_LabelClickThrough()` (D2Client.h/.cpp — `Active() && Latched()`, NEVER true in hold mode) gating THREE sites: the renderer's tail wipe, its mid-loop stale-item revoke, and the UNIT_ITEM exclusion — toggle mode hands selection back to the normal hover machinery (D2R parity: labels stay up, clicks attack/interact, clicking a label or sprite picks the item up); `alt_toggle=0` stays byte-for-byte vanilla at every site. Touched: D2Client.h/.cpp (predicate + one clarifying ini-template comment line), UI/ShowItems.cpp (two gates), UNIT/CUnit.cpp (one gate). Verified: 4 touched TUs compile clean under llvm-mingw i686; full build = the user's local gate. Commit `2ba5b13` (+ a docs commit). Prior rev-11 state: portable-save shipped (`96fbc4f`, saves in `<game folder>\save`, ledger row 30).*

*Rev 13 (2026-10-28, d2boot.log growth fix + banner): **the fix-26/27 diagnostic probes are retired** — field report: "d2boot.log is growing rapidly, we cant have that for 0.03". Census of all 109 `D2BOOT_` call sites found TWO unbounded writers, both pure-logging diag-B scaffolding: **pal26D** (bake stamp, D2Direct3D upload path — the 256-slot DC6-frame-key dedupe table fills within seconds of gameplay, and PAST the cap every text/shifted texture upload wrote a bake line PLUS a synchronous `FlushFileBuffers` on the render thread) and **pal26E** (caller tag in `D2GFX_SetPaletteForDraw` — logged every palette load, and its own field data showed the sky<->fechar ping-pong re-firing every 1-2 s at char select, so the log grew forever while parked at menus). Removed: all five pal26* probes (pal26A ui39 dump, pal26B slot dump, pal26B2 solid dump, pal26D bake/rehit stamp incl. the now-unused `nPal26DFrameKey` capture, pal26E caller tag) — each replaced by a retirement comment; the probes were pure logging (their comments said so) and their missions are complete (fix 27 field-verified). **d2boot.log contract for 0.0.3: boot milestones + D2BOOT_Fail/upload-halt crash triage + fix22 warn-once guards only — zero writes during normal play** (append mode kept: the cross-session trail stays intact for silent-exit triage). Also closed 0.0.2 post-release hygiene item (a): boot banner self-identification is now `vanilla-plus 0.0.3` (was "vanilla-plus fixset through fix 27"). Commit `f10b5ff`. Verified: 5 touched TUs (D2Direct3D/Renderer/Direct3D.cpp, D2Client/Renderer/Direct3D.cpp, D2Win/Src/D2WinPalette.cpp, D2Launch/MainMenus.cpp, D2Client/Engine/Application.cpp) compile clean under llvm-mingw i686; full build = the user's local Win11 gate. ## H22. The ad964f3 merge — supersession map (from handoff §3ter-bis, rev 9, verbatim)

*Verbatim from handoff §3ter-bis, moved at rev 16 (2026-10-28). Companion narrative: H17. The merge itself is DONE and field-verified — this table is the record of which hand fixes upstream absorbed, superseded, or left for vanilla-plus to re-port.*

Resolution discipline: our side = `eef5f2c` + our fixes; their side = `eef5f2c` + `ad964f3` (because `3f6da07` ≡ `eef5f2c`), so every hand-merge ran a 3-way with **`eef5f2c` as base** — conflicts mark exactly where our fixes meet upstream's re-typing. 85 files conflicted; 70 taken wholesale (66 untouched + 4 superseded), 9 auto-merged clean, 7 by hand, `Direct3D.cpp` re-ported onto upstream's tree (`scripts/port_d3d_fixes.py`).

| Our fix | Verdict after ad964f3 | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 (phantom texture-cache structs) | upstream re-typed (in `eef5f2c`, absorbed at `cf36935`) | gone (superseded) |
| 2 / 4 (defects 2+4: class-slot + stale-nodeIdx guards) | **REJECTED upstream, still relink-relevant** | **KEPT** — glide side in `Glide.cpp`, D3D side re-ported as fix 21; §3ter rule: keep until empirically proven unnecessary |
| 3 / 8 (EnsureCapacity results; defects 3+8) | upstream re-typed the returns (`int`) and the miss path | `glTextureCache.cpp` / `glSubtileCache.*` / `glBlocks.cpp` taken from upstream |
| 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 13 (glide defects 5–11) | upstream typings (in `eef5f2c`) | absorbed at `cf36935`; diagnostics (ShadowDiag) kept |
| 15 / 16 (sound, defects 12+13) | upstream never fixed | **KEPT** (`Glide.cpp` clamp auto-merged; `D2SoundSmp.cpp` got upstream's D2_STATIC_GUARDs + our R6 note; `Sound.cpp` fix-16 block intact) |
| 17 (truncated gamma IID) | **superseded**: IID lives as the first 16 bytes of the 272-byte `gszRendererD3DDeviceInitializationCode` blob | upstream kept |
| 18 (gamma fatal → degrade) | **still needed** — upstream QI/ramp failures remain fatal | **re-ported** onto upstream's blob-based QI (consumers null-guarded at :720/:834) |
| 19a/b/d (template[8], GUID, DeviceName buffer) | **superseded**: upstream typed all three (`[8]` with our exact values; `GUID`; `[264]`) | upstream kept |
| 20 (phantom cache statics) | **superseded**: upstream's `pCache = (cache − 0x1c + class·0x1c)` addresses the real pools | upstream kept |
| 21 (class-slot + nodeIdx guards) | **still needed** — upstream still trusts both slots | **re-ported** (markers ×2) |
| 22 (upload-halt dump + hardened free callback) | **still needed** — upstream's 11 halt sites un-instrumented; callback still only signed-checks | **re-ported** (dump def + 12 calls; `D3DTEXCACHE_ClearCacheNodeSlot`) |
| 23 (byte-exact node addressing) | **superseded**: upstream's int-arithmetic (`iCacheNodeIdx − 0x10 + base`) is byte-exact by construction | upstream kept (the `(uint8_t*)pCacheNodeBase` forms are GONE — do not re-add) |
| 24a (ground index arrays) | **still needed** — upstream still has the under-extent phantom triad (now guarded) | **re-ported** (`g_aD3DGroundTileStripIndices[65]` / `g_aD3DGroundTileFanIndices[4]`) |
| 24b (gnPaletteIndexCounter extent) | **superseded, and OUR 40 was off by one**: the loader writes 9 PALETTEENTRY (36B) + a 3-byte RGB triple = **39 B** (the peFlags 4th byte is never written) | upstream's `uint8_t[39]` + guard kept; our comment preserved at the def |
| vanilla-plus-only: CERT_INFO fix, DropDiag/LightDiag/d2boot, banner, tooling, docs | untouched upstream | all survived (14/14 gates) |

New upstream capabilities now in-tree (adopt, don't fight): **guard zones** (`D2_GLOBAL_GUARD`/`D2_STATIC_GUARD` from `d2_platform.h`; `D2LOUD_GuardPump` walks them; a hit logs `D2GUARD <owner> ...` to the d2loud report; `D2_GUARD_HALT=1` makes it fatal; `D2_GUARD_WATCH=<owner>` arms a write watch) — the 39-vs-40 palette question is exactly what the guard would have caught live; **frame groups** (`__frame0_t` where a callee reaches past stack locals); **unnamed data tables restored** (+181k — first full-content rebuild of the .data section; a build from this merge is the first real test).

## H23. Naming collision + pr2 drift (from handoff §2, verbatim)

*Verbatim from handoff §2, moved at rev 16 (2026-10-28). The "fix 13" double-name trap and the pr2-vs-vanilla-plus Glide.cpp drift rule behind per-anchor overlay assertions.*

- **Naming collision — read before confusing yourself.** Three numbering systems circulate:

  | "fix N" (fix-chain) | PR #6 "commit N" | defect (PR body) | What it is |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | fixes 1–7 | commits 1–4 (batch, 6 files, +466/−79) | 1–8 | texture cache (1–4), fan vertices (5–7), subtile halt (8) |
  | fix 8 | commit 5 | — (diagnostic) | LightDiag v1 |
  | fix 9 | commit 6 | 9 | falloff table `[256]`→`[257]` |
  | fix 10 | commit 7 | 10 | ground-argb u32 stores (+21/−9) |
  | fix 11 | commit 8 | — (diagnostic) | ShadowDiag |
  | fix 12 | *no PR change* | — | vanilla-plus-only restore of fix-10 delta |
  | fix 13 (shadowspan) | commit 9 | 11 | DrawShadowQuad portrait spans (+29/−4) |
  | fix 13 (lightcolordiag) | commit 10 | — (diagnostic) | LightDiag v2 |

  Yes, "fix 13" names two different overlays (`pr2-glide-fix13-shadowspan-overlay.zip` / `pr2-glide-fix13-lightcolordiag-overlay.zip`).
- Note: pr2's Glide.cpp text has **drifted from vanilla-plus's** (jaenster revision, ~2441 lines). Overlay scripts therefore assert *per-anchor* uniqueness, never whole-file equality, across the two lines.


## H24. handoff.md — third offload: pre-720p content (revs 14–27 + §1–§13, verbatim)

*Verbatim from handoff.md, moved at rev 28 (2026-10-30) when the handoff was refocused on the 720p effort (owner: the branch exists for the 1280x720 work). Covers the vanilla-plus feature era through 0.0.5: options-label rename, persistent globe text, per-class sound tables restored from the retail exe, the inventory-full pickup crash, the alt-toggle stale-selection fix, the options-page field-QA rounds, the repo restructure, the 0.0.3 release pass, and the standing sections (topology, fix-chain ledger, sound series, upstream verdict, methodology, delivery conventions, diagnostics, tooling, field loop, triage, sandbox gotchas). The 720p work itself starts at handoff rev 28; its engineering record lives in docs/resolution-widelayout-audit.md.*

*Rev 27 (2026-10-30, options row label rename "Alt-Show Items" → "Alt Item Toggle" — owner: "just change the name of the label on the menu"): label-only round, zero feature/code-surface changes (key `alt_toggle`, statics, handlers, gates all untouched). Rationale: ledger row 28's feature is D2R "Show Items: Toggle" parity — each Alt press LATCHES ground-item-label visibility (rows 31/23 add click-through + stale-selection fixes) — while the old label read as hold-Alt-shows-items (vanilla semantics), so the accurate noun is a toggle. Changed: `gaVanillaPlusRows[0].wszLabel` in VanillaPlusOptions.cpp (the only user-facing surface; row draws the label raw at :585, On/Off is a separate draw, and "Persistent Globe Text" at 21 chars already proves width headroom for the 15-char new label) + the one stale draft-doc example (DRAFT_vanilla_plus_options.md §A listing `Alt-Show Items: On/Off`). The auto-created ini template comment was already accurate ("press Alt once to toggle labels (D2R \"Show Items: Toggle\")") and stays. Gates unchanged/green. Remaining: owner rebuild + glance at the Esc page row text → ledger row 34 cosmetic addendum.*

*Rev 26 (2026-10-30, orb-face globe readout removed, slate row 1 repurposed as persistent orb text — patch 0023): owner field round on 0.0.4: "the health globe hover txt shows both above and on the globe itself. just above is fine. we dont need the duplicate display", then the pivot: "1.14d retail already displayed above on hover... change the Globe Hover feature to a 'Persistent' toggle instead... we dont have to hover to see retails above globe text". In-tree confirmation: retail's PANEL_DrawPanelLayout (Panel.cpp 1272/1291) already prints the same cur/max ABOVE each orb on hover — locale 0x1045/0x1046, baseline ScreenSizeY-0x5f (~10 px above the orb top ≈ ScreenSizeY-0x55), hover rect x 0x1e..0x6f / ScreenSizeY-0x4b..-0xf — and retail even ships its own always-on mechanism: bShowHpText/bShowmpText ("Show HP Text"/"Show MP Text" registry values, SRegLoadValue at Panel.cpp 148-149, flipped by the retail options click path at 2111/2129), ||'d ahead of the hover test. Our 0.0.4 orb-face copy (baseline ScreenSizeY-0x2d, mid-orb) was therefore a second readout of the same numbers. Fix, one commit: (1) VANILLAPLUS_DrawGlobeValues + statics + decl + DRAW_UI VP block deleted; (2) the slate row is repurposed as VANILLAPLUS_GlobeTextPersistent() — the house 2 s gate over [features] globe_persistent (renamed from globe_values; a stale globe_values=1 line in an existing d2vp.ini is inert, nothing reads it) — ||'d into BOTH retail draw conditions (2 VP-marked one-liners, precedence-safe between the || terms; hover and retail's own registry toggle keep working unchanged); (3) options page row relabeled "Persistent Globe Text" (VPP_ROW_COUNT stays 4); (4) ini template globe_persistent block; (5) gates: 5 globe gates → 6 persistent gates (fn defined, ini key, decl+cache-bust, Panel wiring x2, fork-api include in Panel.cpp — Panel.cpp joins the explicit-include list), 66→67; (6) docs: FEATURES row 1 + §1 record, VANILLA-PLUS bullet, README both feature lists. No Panel.cpp draw logic changed beyond the two || terms — the hover text is byte-for-byte retail when globe_persistent=0. Remaining: owner rebuild + field round (persistent text with and without hover, retail toggle coexistence, options row flip) → ledger row 40.*

*Rev 25 (2026-10-29, per-class player sound tables restored from retail — Rev 24 follow-up, patches 0020+0021): owner field round on `94459d6`: "Great job, crash fixed. It doesn't play the voice, but it does play the item flipping sound" — exactly the recorded Rev 24 follow-up (zero-filled tables = class event sounds silent; the flip is a hardcoded SOUND_ITEM_* id, not table-driven). Owner then supplied the retail 1.14d Game.exe (sha256 `631066c1…8adaaf`, PE32, 2016-05-31), which settles the recovery question: the tables are STATIC .data, never runtime-filled. The decompile's `bUnitSoundTableInit` 1-byte stub at .data 0x727eac is `gaUnitSoundIdsAmazon[0]`'s low byte, the six sibling stubs are the first bytes of the contiguous 0x729f00..0x72a008 block, and `gaUnitSoundTableByClassId` itself is .data 0x72a008 with entry order [Ama,Sorc,Nec,Pal,Barb,Dru,Ass] matching the decompiled initializer. All 7x11 values read out of the file and decode against the LoD sounds.txt row order (1.13d excel, same rows): [0]/[1] hit/death bases, [2..7] event barks 0x13 impossible / 0x15 needmana / 0x16 needkey / 0x17 cantcarry (the pickup-fail voice) / 0x14 cantuseyet / 0x18 not_in_town — every wav basename matches its server-side AttachEventSound trigger (chest ops→needkey, gold-overflow/corpse→cantcarry) — [8] chat_a_1 for anims 0x19-0x20, [9] act1_complete_andariel with quest events 0x21-0x53 linear (0x53 = [9]+0x32 = act5_defeat_baal, matching the A5Q sends), [10] footstep blocks (barb heavy / ama+pal medium / sor+nec+dru+ass light). Fix (`24a8bf6`): the seven arrays become initialized .data with the retail values, the phantom flag byte is removed, comments updated; the sounds.txt null guard stays (defense in depth). TRAP for the future: the sound-name enum in d2_enums.h is the PRE-LoD sounds.txt — LoD rebuilt the table with inserted rows, so classic names misattribute everything from ~2263 up; never use them to name LoD rows. Gates 57→66 (per-class full-initializer tripwires + phantom removal). Remaining: owner rebuild + field round (pickup-fail voice per class, quest fanfares, hit/death, footsteps) → ledger row 39.*

*Rev 24 (2026-10-29, inventory-full pickup crash — sound-defect fix, patches 0018+0019): owner field report: "if i try to pick up an item when the inventory is full, the game crashes" + the VEH console log (AV READ at 0x3C, EIP 001BDD0E inside the exe) and the full d2crash report. Stack symbolised against the owner's own d2exe.map — the map is 0x400000-based while the live exe ASLR-relocated to 0x130000, so map VA = RVA + 0x400000; owner build id `c81aae6` is not on the sandbox clone's history (local-commit lineage), but the touched TUs are patch-free, so codegen matches. Resolved stack: `SOUND_PlaySound`+0x3D ← `UNITSND_PlayPlayerSound`+0x71E ← `UNITSND_PlaySoundForMercenary`+0x6A8 ← `NET_D2GS_CLIENT_Incoming0x2C_PLAY_SOUND` ← packet loop. NOT the pickup code at all: the "can't pick that up" feedback is the server's 0x2C event-sound packet (the `SetupUpdateEvent_AttachEventSound` 0x17 family — ItemMode gold-overflow 1008, PlayerModes corpse 686, etc.). Two legs, both verified in-tree: (1) `SOUND_PlaySound` derefed the sounds.txt line unchecked — `TXT_sounds_GetLineBySoundId` returns nullptr for any id outside [0, nTxtSoundsSize), and the register set matches line 452's `pSoundsTxt->Volume` exactly (Volume@0x3C per the packed struct, EAX=0, fault at +0x3D = first deref at -O0); (2) the id feeding it was garbage by construction — the per-class player sound tables `gaUnitSoundIds*` were decompiled as 1-byte BSS stubs (retail fills them at runtime behind `bUnitSoundTableInit`; that init is unrecovered — zero writers, zero readers in-tree) while `UNITSND_PlayPlayerSound` / `UNITSND_GetFootstepSound` / `UNITSND_PlayHitOrDeathSound` index [0]..[10] — every read ran past the object into linker-adjacent globals. Fix (`94459d6`): null guard at the lookup (silent no-op — closes the whole crash class, since no channel is ever created for an unresolvable id, every downstream sounds.txt deref only ever sees known lines) + the seven tables right-sized to zero-filled `int[11]` (zero = SOUND_NONE = every consumer's existing early-out: [0]/[1] hit-death guard at 2078, [2..7] dedupe gate at 1601, [10] footstep `!nFootstepSound` at 747) + pointer-table entry 0 (Amazon) retargeted from the orphaned flag byte to a real table. 4 new grep gates = 57. Follow-up (recorded, not scheduled): recover the retail init / real table contents from the 1.14d exe — until then class hit/death/event sounds keyed on these tables are silent and quest-fanfare ids via the [9] base arithmetic land on deterministic small ids (pre-existing wrongness, now deterministic). Remaining: owner rebuild + field round (reported scenario + normal play) → ledger row 38.*

*Rev 23 (2026-10-29, alt-toggle stale-selection fix — field bug, patches 0016+0017): owner field report: with labels latched ON, standing on an item and walk-clicking far away picked the item up ("sometimes"). Root cause chain (all three legs verified in-tree): (1) the label-hover select `SELECT_UpdateUnitSelection` (ShowItems mid-loop, retail fn) PINS the selection — `bCanSelectUnit = 1` — and `SetSelectedUnit_I` refuses every other setter while pinned, the per-frame machinery (`SELECT_UpdateSelectedUnit`) no-ops at its lock check; (2) `GetSelectedUnit` re-validates ON-SCREEN ONLY (`HitTestScreen(pUnit, 0, 0, nullptr)`), so a pinned on-screen item never self-clears — retail hold-Alt never notices because its `HitTestScreen` UNIT_ITEM exclusion makes item selections self-invalidate there, and row 31 lifted exactly that exclusion for toggle mode; (3) row 31 also skipped vanilla's renderer tail wipe in toggle mode, the wipe that would have unloaded the stale selection. Net: a label-hover selection stuck forever; `ClickMap` then re-targets ANY click to it — and rewrites the click coords to the item's position (Player.cpp 2065-2068) — so a far walk click became walk-to-item + pickup (instant when standing on it). Fix (ShowItems.cpp only, hold mode byte-for-byte — the added clauses are dead when `ALTTOGGLE_LabelClickThrough()` is false): toggle mode now runs vanilla's mid-loop revoke when the cursor is over NEITHER the selected item's label rect NOR its sprite (rect-miss + sprite-miss → `ClearSelectedUnit` → unpinned; the selection follows the cursor label-to-label again), and the tail (cursor over no label rect) unloads a ground-item selection the cursor is not over by sprite hit — vanilla's blanket wipe would have killed the field-proven sprite-click surface (the committed label rect stops at the item's Y; the sprite extends below it), so the sprite hit (`HitTestScreen` with real cursor coords, retail hover idiom) spares it. Non-item selections (monsters etc.) are unpinned by construction and self-heal per frame — untouched. Costs one `HitTestScreen` per frame max, only while labels are latched. All toggle semantics preserved: label click picks up, sprite click picks up, click elsewhere walks, hold-Alt untouched. Gates unchanged (x2 call sites); allowlist ShowItems 18→44. Remaining: owner rebuild + field round (the reported scenario + label/sprite clicks + combat clicks) → ledger row 37.
